\tWebauthn    *types.Webauthn\n67: \tidentity    loginIdentity\n68: \tsessionData sessionIdentity\n69: }\n70: \n71: func isReuseAllowedForScope(scope mfav1.ChallengeScope) bool {\n72: \tswitch scope {\n73: \tcase mfav1.ChallengeScope_CHALLENGE_SCOPE_ADMIN_ACTION,\n74: \t\tmfav1.ChallengeScope_CHALLENGE_SCOPE_USER_SESSION:\n75: \t\treturn true\n76: \tdefault:\n77: \t\treturn false\n78: \t}\n79: }\n80: \n81: func (f *loginFlow) begin(ctx context.Context, params BeginParams) (*wantypes.CredentialAssertion, error) {\n82: \tif params.ChallengeExtensions == nil {\n83: \t\treturn nil, trace.BadParameter(\"requested challenge extensions must be supplied.\")\n84: \t}\n85: \n86: \tif params.ChallengeExtensions.AllowReuse == mfav1.ChallengeAllowReuse_CHALLENGE_ALLOW_REUSE_YES && !isReuseAllowedForScope(params.ChallengeExtensions.Scope) {\n87: \t\treturn nil, trace.BadParameter(\"mfa challenges with scope %s cannot allow reuse\", params.ChallengeExtensions.Scope)\n88: \t}\n89: \n90: \t// discoverableLogin identifies logins started with an unknown/empty user.\n91: \tdiscoverableLogin := params.ChallengeExtensions.Scope == mfav1.ChallengeScope_CHALLENGE_SCOPE_PASSWORDLESS_LOGIN\n92: \tif params.User == \"\" && !discoverableLogin {\n93: \t\treturn nil, trace.BadParameter(\"user required\")\n94: \t}\n95: \n96: \tvar u \t\t\treturn nil, trace.Wrap(err)\n103: \t\t}\n104: \n105: \t\t// Use existing devices to set the allowed credentials.\n106: \t\tdevices, err := f.identity.GetMFADevices(ctx, params.User, false /* withSecrets */)\n107: \t\tif err != nil {\n108: \t\t\treturn nil, trace.Wrap(err)\n109: \t\t}\n110: \n111: \t\t// Filter devices with the wrong RPID and log an error.\n112: \t\tfoundInvalid := false\n113: \t\tfor i := 0; i < len(devices); i++ {\n114: \t\t\twebDev := devices[i].GetWebauthn()\n115: \t\t\tif webDev == nil || webDev.CredentialRpId == \"\" || webDev.CredentialRpId == f.Webauthn.RPID {\n116: \t\t\t\tcontinue\n117: \t\t\t}\n118: \n119: \t\t\tconst msg = \"User device has unexpected RPID, excluding from allowed credentials. \" +\n120: \t\t\t\t\"RPID changes are not supported by WebAuthn, this is likely to cause permanent authentication problems for your users. \" +\n121: \t\t\t\t\"Consider reverting the change or reset your users so they may register their devices again.\"\n122: \t\t\tlog.ErrorContext(ctx, msg,\n123: \t\t\t\t\"user\", params.User,\n124: \t\t\t\t\"device\", devices[i].GetName(),\n125: \t\t\t\t\"rpid\", webDev.CredentialRpId,\n126: \t\t\t)\n127: \n128: \t\t\t// \"Cut\" device from slice.\n129: \t\t\tdevices = slices.Delete(devices, i, i+1)\n130: \t\t\ti--\n131: \n132: \t\t\tfoundInvalid = true\n133: \t\t}\n134: \n135: \t\t// Sort non-resident keys first, which may cause clients to favor them for\n136: \t\t// MFA in some scenarios (eg, tsh).\n137: \t\tsort.Slice(devices, func(i, j int) bool {\n138: \t\t\tdev1, dev2 := devices[i], devices[j]\n139: \t\t\tweb1, web2 := dev1.GetWebauthn(), dev2.GetWebauthn()\n140: \t\t\tresident1 := web1 != nil && web1.ResidentKey\n141: \t\t\tresident2 := web2 != nil && web2.ResidentKey\n142: \t\t\treturn !resident1 && resident2\n143: \t\t})\n144: \n145: \t\tu = newWebUser(webUserOpts{\n146: \t\t\tname:             params.User,\n147: \t\t\twebID:            webID,\n148: \t\t\tdevices:          devices,\n149: \t\t\tcredentialIDOnly: true,\n150: \t\t})\n151: \n152: \t\t// Let's make sure we have at least one registered credential here, since we\n153: \t\t// have to allow zero credentials for passwordless below.\n154: \t\tif len(u.credentials) == 0 {\n155: \t\t\tif foundInvalid {\n156: \t\t\t\treturn nil, trace.Wrap(ErrInvalidCredentials)\n157: \t\t\t}\n158: \t\t\treturn nil, trace.NotFound(\"found no credentials for user %q\", params.User)\n159: \t\t}\n160: \t}\n161: \n162: \t// TODO(codingllama): Use the \"official\" appid impl by duo-labs/webauthn.\n163: \tvar opts []wan.LoginOption\n164: \tif f.U2F != nil && f.U2F.AppID != \"\" {\n165: \t\t// See https://www.w3.org/TR/webauthn-2/#sctn-appid-extension.\n166: \t\topts = append(opts, wan.WithAssertionExtensions(protocol.AuthenticationExtensions{\n167: \t\t\twantypes.AppIDExtension: f.U2F.AppID,\n168: \t\t}))\n169: \t}\n170: \t// Set the user verification requirement, if present, only for\n171: \t// non-discoverable logins.\n172: \t// For discoverable logins we rely on the wan.WebAuthn default set below.\n173: \tif !discoverableLogin && params.ChallengeExtensions.UserVerificationRequirement != \"\" {\n174: \t\tuvr := protocol.UserVerificationRequirement(params.ChallengeExtensions.UserVerificationRequirement)\n175: \t\topts = append(opts, wan.WithUserVerification(uvr))\n176: \t}\n177: \n178: \t// Create the WebAuthn object and issue a new challenge.\n179: \tweb, err := newWebAuthn(webAuthnParams{\n180: \t\tcfg:                     f.Webauthn,\n181: \t\trpID:                    f.Webauthn.RPID,\n182: \t\trequireUserVerification: discoverableLogin,\n183: \t})\n184: \tif err != nil {\n185: \t\treturn nil, trace.Wrap(err)\n186: \t}\n187: \n188: \tvar assertion *protocol.CredentialAssertion\n189: \tvar sessionData *wan.SessionData\n190: \tif discoverableLogin {\n191: \t\tassertion, sessionData, err = web.BeginDiscoverableLogin(opts...)\n192: \t} else {\n193: \t\tassertion, sessionData, err = web.BeginLogin(u, opts...)\n194: \t}\n195: \tif err != nil {\n196: \t\treturn nil, trace.Wrap(err)\n197: \t}\n198: \n199: \t// Store SessionData - it's checked against the user response by Finish.\n200: \tsd, err := wantypes.SessionDataFromProtocol(sessionData)\n201: \tif err != nil {\n202: \t\treturn       params.ChallengeExtensions.AllowReuse,\n207: \t\tUserVerificationRequirement: params.ChallengeExtensions.UserVerificationRequirement,\n208: \t}\n209: \n210: \t// Attach SIP if provided.\n211: \n227: func (f *loginFlow) getWebID(ctx context.Context, user string) ([]byte, error) {\n228: \twla, err := f.identity.GetWebauthnLocalAuth(ctx, user)\n229: \tswitch {\n230: \tcase trace.IsNotFound(err):\n231: \t\treturn nil, nil // OK, legacy U2F users may not have a webID.\n232: \tcase err != nil:\n233: \t\treturn nil, trace.Wrap(err)\n234: \t}\n235: \treturn wla.UserID, nil\n236: }\n237: \n238: // LoginData is data gathered from a successful webauthn login.\n239: type LoginData struct {\n240: \t// User is the Teleport user.\n241: \tUser string\n242: \t// Device is the MFA device used to authenticate the user.\n243: \tDevice *types.MFADevice\n244: \t// AllowReuse is whether the webauthn challenge used for this login\n245: \t// can be reused by the user for subsequent logins, until it expires.\n246: \tAllowReuse mfav1.ChallengeAllowReuse\n247: \t// Payload is the optional session identifying payload to attach to the login.\n248: \tPayload *mfatypes.SessionIdentifyingPayload\n249: \t// SourceCluster is the source cluster name associated with this login.\n250: \tSourceCluster string\n251: \t// TargetCluster is the target cluster name associated with this login.\n252: \tTargetCluster string\n253: }\n254: \n255: func (f *loginFlow) finish(ctx context.Context, user string, resp *wantypes.CredentialAssertionResponse, requiredExtensions *mfav1.ChallengeExtensions) (*LoginData, error) {\n256: \tif requiredExtensions == nil {\n257: \t\treturn nil, trace.BadParameter(\"requested challenge extensions must be supplied.\")\n258: \t}\n259: \n260: \tdiscoverableLogin := requiredExtensions.Scope == mfav1.ChallengeScope_CHALLENGE_SCOPE_PASSWORDLESS_LOGIN\n261: \n262: \tswitch {\n263: \tcase user == \"\" && !discoverableLogin:\n264: \t\treturn nil, trace.BadParameter(\"user required\")\n265: \tcase resp == nil:\n266: \t\t// resp != nil is good enough to proceed, we leave remaining validations to\n267: \t\t// duo-labs/webauthn.\n268: \t\treturn nil, trace.BadParameter(\"credential assertion response required\")\n269: \t}\n270: \n271: \tparsedResp, err := parseCredentialResponse(resp)\n272: \tif err != nil {\n273: \t\treturn nil, trace.Wrap(err)\n274: \t}\n275: \n276: \torigin := parsedResp.Response.CollectedClientData.Origin\n277: \tif err := validateOrigin(origin, f.Webauthn.RPID); err != nil {\n278: \t\tlog.DebugContext(ctx, \"origin validation failed\", \"error\", err)\n279: \t\treturn nil, trace.Wrap(err)\n280: \t}\n281: \n282: \tvar webID []byte\n283: \tif discoverableLogin {\n284: \t\twebID = parsedResp.Response.UserHandle\n285: \t\tif len(webID) == 0 {\n286: \t\t\treturn nil, trace.BadParameter(\"webauthn user handle required for passwordless\")\n287: \t\t}\n288: \n289: \t\t// Fetch user from WebAuthn UserHandle (aka User ID).\n290: \t\tteleportUser, err := f.identity.GetTeleportUserByWebauthnID(ctx, webID)\n291: \t\tif err != nil {\n292: \t\t\treturn nil, trace.Wrap(err)\n293: \t\t}\n294: \t\tuser = teleportUser\n295: \t} else {\n296: \t\twebID, err = f.getWebID(ctx, user)\n297: \t\tif err != nil {\n298: \t\t\treturn nil, trace.Wrap(err)\n299: \t\t}\n300: \t}\n301: \n302: \t// Find the device used to sign the credentials. It must be a previously\n303: \t// registered device.\n304: \tdevices, err := f.identity.GetMFADevices(ctx, user, false /* withSecrets */)\n305: \tif err != nil {\n306: \t\treturn nil, trace.Wrap(err)\n307: \t}\n308: \tdev, ok := findDeviceByID(devices, parsedResp.RawID)\n309: \tif !ok {\n310: \t\treturn nil, trace.BadParameter(\n311: \t\t\t\"unknown device credential: %q\", base64.RawURLEncoding.EncodeToString(parsedResp.RawID))\n312: \t}\n313: \n314: \t// Is an U2F device trying to login? If yes, use RPID = App ID.\n315: \t// Technically browsers should reply with the appid extension set to true[1],\n316: \t// but in actuality they don't send anything.\n317: \t// [1] https://www.w3.org/TR/webauthn-2/#sctn-appid-extension.\n318: \trpID := f.Webauthn.RPID\n319: \tswitch {\n320: \tcase dev.GetU2F() != nil && f.U2F == nil:\n321: \t\treturn nil, trace.BadParameter(\"U2F device attempted login, but U2F configuration not present\")\n322: \tcase dev.GetU2F() != nil:\n323: \t\trpID = f.U2F.AppID\n324: \t}\n325: \tu := newWebUser(webUserOpts{\n326: \t\tname:    user,\n327: \t\twebID:   webID,\n328: \t\tdevices: []*types.MFADevice{dev},\n329: \t\tcurrentFlags: &credentialFlags{\n330: \t\t\tBE: parsedResp.Response.AuthenticatorData.Flags.HasBackupEligible(),\n331: \t\t\tBS: parsedResp.Response.AuthenticatorData.Flags.HasBackupState(),\n332: \t\t},\n333: \t})\n334: \n335: \t// Fetch the previously-stored SessionData, so it's checked against the user\n336: \t// response.\n337: \tchallenge := parsedResp.Response.CollectedClientData.Challenge\n338: \tsd, err := f.sessionData.Get(ctx, user, challenge)\n339: \tif err != nil {\n340: \t\treturn nil, trace.Wrap(err)\n341: \t}\n342: \n343: \t// Check if the given scope is satisfied by the challenge scope.\n344: \tif requiredExtensions.Scope != sd.ChallengeExtensions.Scope && requiredExtensions.Scope != mfav1.ChallengeScope_CHALLENGE_SCOPE_UNSPECIFIED {\n345: \t\treturn nil, trace.AccessDenied(\"required scope %q is not satisfied by the given webauthn session with scope %q\", requiredExtensions.Scope, sd.ChallengeExtensions.Scope)\n346: \t}\n347: \n348: \tnoReuseAllowed := requiredExtensions.AllowReuse == mfav1.ChallengeAllowReuse_CHALLENGE_ALLOW_REUSE_NO\n349: \tchallengeAllowReuse := sd.ChallengeExtensions.AllowReuse == mfav1.ChallengeAllowReuse_CHALLENGE_ALLOW_REUSE_YES\n350: \n351: \t// If this session is reusable, but this login forbids reusable sessions, return an error.\n352: \tif noReuseAllowed && challengeAllowReuse {\n353: \t\treturn nil, trace.AccessDenied(\"the given webauthn session allows reuse, but reuse is not permitted in this context\")\n354: \t}\n355: \n356: \t// Verify (and possibly correct) the user verification requirement.\n357: \t// A mismatch here could indicate a programming error or even foul play.\n358: \tuvr := protocol.UserVerificationRequirement(requiredExtensions.UserVerificationRequirement)\n359: \tif (discoverableLogin || uvr == protocol.VerificationRequired) && sd.UserVerification != string(protocol.VerificationRequired) {\n360: \t\t// This is not a failure yet, but will likely become one.\n361: \t\tsd.UserVerification = string(protocol.VerificationRequired)\n362: \t\tconst msg = \"User verification required by extensions but not by challenge. \" +\n363: \t\t\t\"Increased SessionData.UserVerification.\"\n364: \t\tlog.WarnContext(ctx, msg, \"user_verification\", sd.UserVerification)\n365: \t}\n366: \n367: \tsessionData := wantypes.SessionDataToProtocol(sd)\n368: \n369: \t// Make sure _all_ credentials in the session are accounted for by the user.\n370: \t// webauthn.ValidateLogin requires it.\n371: \tfor _, allowedCred := range sessionData.AllowedCredentialIDs {\n372: \t\tif bytes.Equal(parsedResp.RawID, allowedCred) {\n373: \t\t\tcontinue\n374: \t\t}\n375: \t\tu.credentials = append(u.credentials, wan.Credential{\n376: \t\t\tID: allowedCred,\n377: \t\t})\n378: \t}\n379: \n380: \t// Create a WebAuthn matching the expected RPID and Origin, then verify the\n381: \t// signed challenge.\n382: \tweb, err := newWebAuthn(webAuthnParams{\n383: \t\tcfg:                     f.Webauthn,\n384: \t\trpID:                    rpID,\n385: \t\torigin:                  origin,\n386: \t\trequireUserVerification: discoverableLogin,\n387: \t})\n388: \tif err != nil {\n389: \t\treturn nil, trace.Wrap(err)\n390: \t}\n391: \n392: \tvar credential *wan.Credential\n393: \tif discoverableLogin {\n394: \t\tdiscoverUser := func(_, _ []byte) (wan.User, error) { return u, nil }\n395: \t\tcredential, err = web.ValidateDiscoverableLogin(discoverUser, *sessionData, parsedResp)\n396: \t} else {\n397: \t\tcredential, err = web.ValidateLogin(u, *sessionData, parsedResp)\n398: \t}\n399: \tif err != nil {\n400: \t\treturn nil, trace.Wrap(err)\n401: \t}\n402: \tif credential.Authenticator.CloneWarning {\n403: \t\t// Reused challenges trigger clone warnings because, after first use, we expect\n404: \t\t// the counter to be up by one.\n405: \t\tisReuseCounterMismatch := challengeAllowReuse &&\n406: \t\t\tlen(u.credentials) == 1 /* sanity check */ &&\n407: \t\t\tcredential.Authenticator.SignCount != u.credentials[0].Authenticator.SignCount-1\n408: \t\tif !isReuseCounterMismatch {\n409: \t\t\tlog.WarnContext(ctx, \"Clone warning detected for device, the device counter may be malfunctioning\",\n410: \t\t\t\t\"user\", user,\n411: \t\t\t\t\"device\", dev.GetName(),\n412: \t\t\t)\n413: \t\t}\n414: \t}\n415: \n416: \t// Update last used timestamp and device counter.\n417: \tif err := updateCredentialAndTimestamps(dev, credential, discoverableLogin); err != nil {\n418: \t\treturn nil, trace.Wrap(err)\n419: \t}\n420: \t// Retroactively write the credential RPID, now that it cleared authn.\n421: \tif webDev := dev.GetWebauthn(); webDev != nil && webDev.CredentialRpId == \"\" {\n422: \t\tlog.DebugContext(ctx, \"Recording RPID in device\",\n423: \t\t\t\"rpid\", rpID,\n424: \t\t\t\"user\", user,\n425: \t\t\t\"device\", dev.GetName(),\n426: \t\t)\n427: \t\twebDev.CredentialRpId = rpID\n428: \t}\n429: \n430: \tif err := f.identity.UpsertMFADevice(ctx, user, dev); err != nil {\n431: \t\treturn nil, trace.Wrap(err)\n432: \t}\n433: \n434: \t// The user just solved the challenge, so let's make sure it won't be used\n435: \t// again, unless reuse is explicitly allowed.\n436: \t// Note that even reusable sessions are deleted when their expiration time\n437: \t// passes.\n438: \tif !challengeAllowReuse {\n439: \t\tif err := f.sessionData.Delete(ctx, user, challenge); err != nil {\n440: \t\t\tlog.WarnContext(ctx, \"failed to delete login SessionData for user\",\n441: \t\t\t\t\"user\", user,\n442: \t\t\t\t\"scope\", sd.ChallengeExtensions.Scope,\n443: \t\t\t)\n444: \t\t}\n445: \t}\n446: \n447: \treturn &LoginData{\n448: \t\tUser:          user,\n449: \t\tDevice:        dev,\n450: \t\tAllowReuse:    sd.ChallengeExtensions.AllowReuse,\n451: \t\tPayload:       sd.Payload,\n452: \t\tSourceCluster: sd.SourceCluster,\n453: \t\tTargetCluster: sd.TargetCluster,\n454: \t}, nil\n455: }\n456: \n457: func parseCredentialResponse(resp *wantypes.CredentialAssertionResponse) (*protocol.ParsedCredentialAssertionData, error) {\n458: \t// Do not pass extensions on to duo-labs/webauthn, they won't go past JSON\n459: \t// unmarshal.\n460: \texts := resp.Extensions\n461: \tresp.Extensions = nil\n462: \tdefer func() { resp.Extensions = exts }()\n463: \n464: \t// This is a roundabout way of getting resp validated, but unfortunately the\n465: \t// APIs don't provide a better method (and it seems better than duplicating\n466: \t// library code here).\n467: \tbody, err := json.Marshal(resp)\n468: \tif err != nil {\n469: \t\treturn nil, trace.Wrap(err)\n470: \t}\n471: \treturn protocol.ParseCredentialRequestResponseBody(bytes.NewReader(body))\n472: }\n473: \n474: func findDeviceByID(devices []*types.MFADevice, id []byte) (*types.MFADevice, bool) {\n475: \tfor _, dev := range devices {\n476: \t\tswitch d := dev.Device.(type) {\n477: \t\tcase *types.MFADevice_U2F:\n478: \t\t\tif bytes.Equal(d.U2F.KeyHandle, id) {\n479: \t\t\t\treturn dev, true\n480: \t\t\t}\n481: \t\tcase *types.MFADevice_Webauthn:\n482: \t\t\tif bytes.Equal(d.Webauthn.CredentialId, id) {\n483: \t\t\t\treturn dev, true\n484: \t\t\t}\n485: \t\t}\n486: \t}\n487: \treturn nil, false\n488: }\n489: \n490: func updateCredentialAndTimestamps(\n491: \tdest *types.MFADevice,\n492: \tcredential *wan.Credential,\n493: \tdiscoverableLogin bool,\n494: ) error {\n495: \tswitch d := dest.Device.(type) {\n496: \tcase *types.MFADevice_U2F:\n497: \t\td.U2F.Counter = credential.Authenticator.SignCount\n498: \n499: \tcase *types.MFADevice_Webauthn:\n500: \t\td.Webauthn.SignatureCounter = credential.Authenticator.SignCount\n501: \n502: \t\t// Backfill ResidentKey field.\n503: \t\t// This may happen if an authenticator created for \"MFA\" was actually\n504: \t\t// resident all along (eg, Safari/Touch ID registrations).\n505: \t\tif discoverableLogin && !d.Webauthn.ResidentKey {\n506: \t\t\td.Webauthn.ResidentKey = true\n507: \t\t}\n508: \n509: \t\t// Backfill BE/BS bits.\n510: \t\tif d.Webauthn.CredentialBackupEligible == nil {\n511: \t\t\td.Webauthn.CredentialBackupEligible = &gogotypes.BoolValue{\n512: \t\t\t\tValue: credential.Flags.BackupEligible,\n513: \t\t\t}\n514: \t\t\tlog.DebugContext(context.Background(), \"Backfilled issue where users with multiple U2F hardware tokens\n// only get a challenge for a single device during login, preventing them from using\n// their other registered devices.\nfunc TestRegression_MultiDeviceU2FAuthenticationChallenge(t *testing.T) {\n\tctx := context.Background()\n\n\t// Create two separate U2F devices\n\tu2fKey1, err := mocku2f.Create()\n\trequire.NoError(t, err)\n\tu2fKey1.SetCounter(10)\n\n\tu2fKey2, err := mocku2f.Create()\n\trequire.NoError(t, err)\n\tu2fKey2.SetCounter(20)\n\n\t// Convert mock keys to MFA devices\n\tdev1 := keyToU2FMFADevice(\"u2f-device-1\", u2fKey1, time.Now().Add(-5*time.Minute))\n\tdev2 := keyToU2FMFADevice(\"u2f-device-2\", u2fKey2, time.Now().Add(-5*time.Minute))\n\n\t// Create identity with both devices registered\n\tidentity := newTestIdentity(\"testuser\", dev1, dev2)\n\n\t// Create login flow with U2F and WebAuthn config\n\tu2fConfig := &types.U2F{AppID: \"https://example.com:3080\"}\n\twebConfig := &types.Webauthn{RPID: \"example.com\"}\n\n\twebLogin := &webauthn.LoginFlow{\n\t\tU2F:      u2fConfig,\n\t\tWebauthn: webConfig,\n\t\tIdentity: identity,\n\t}\n\n\t// Test: Begin login challenge generation\n\t// This should create a challenge with credentials for BOTH devices\n\tassertion, err := webLogin.Begin(ctx, webauthn.BeginParams{\n\t\tUser: \"testuser\",\n\t\tChallengeExtensions: &testChallengeExtensions{\n\t\t\tscope: \"login\",\n\t\t},\n\t})\n\trequire.NoError(t, err, \"Failed to begin login challenge\")\n\trequire.NotNil(t, assertion, \"Assertion should not be nil\")\n\n\t// Extract allowed credential IDs from the assertion\n\tallowedCredentials := assertion.Response.AllowedCredentials\n\trequire.NotEmpty(t, allowedCredentials, \"Allowed credentials should not be empty\")\n\n\t// BUG: This test will FAIL if only one device is in the challenge\n\t// The regression is that allowedCredentials contains only 1 ID instead of 2\n\trequire.Len(t, allowedCredentials, 2,\n\t\t\"Challenge should include credentials for BOTH registered U2F devices. \"+\n\t\t\t\"BUG: Only %d device(s) present in challenge instead of 2. \"+\n\t\t\t\"This prevents users from choosing which device to use.\", len(allowedCredentials))\n\n\t// Verify that both device key handles are in the allowed credentials\n\tkeyHandle1 := u2fKey1.KeyHandle\n\tkeyHandle2 := u2fKey2.KeyHandle\n\tvar foundKey1, foundKey2 bool\n\n\tfor _, cred := range allowedCredentials {\n\t\tif len(cred.CredentialID) > 0 {\n\t\t\t// Check if this credential ID matches either device's key handle\n\t\t\tif string(cred.CredentialID) == string(keyHandle1) {\n\t\t\t\tfoundKey1 = true\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t\tif string(cred.CredentialID) == string(keyHandle2) {\n\t\t\t\tfoundKey2 = true\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\n\trequire.True(t, foundKey1, \"First U2F device key handle not found in challenge credentials\")\n\trequire.True(t, foundKey2, \"Second U2F device key handle not found in challenge credentials\")\n}\n\n// TestRegression_MultiDeviceU2FWebAuthnCompatibility tests that both old and new\n// clients can properly negotiate multi-device U2F challenges.\nfunc TestRegression_MultiDeviceU2FWebAuthnCompatibility(t *testing.T) {\n\tctx := context.Background()\n\n\t// Create a mocku2f.Create()\n\trequire.NoError(t, err)\n\tu2fKey2.SetCounter(20)\n\n\t// Create WebAuthn device (newer standard)\n\twebKey, err := mocku2f.Create()\n\trequire.NoError(t, err)\n\twebKey.PreferRPID = true\n\twebKey.SetCounter(30)\n\n\tdev1 := keyToU2FMFADevice(\"u2f-device-1\", u2fKey1, time.Now().Add(-5*time.Minute))\n\tdev2 := keyToU2FMFADevice(\"u2f-device-2\", u2fKey2, time.Now().Add(-5*time.Minute))\n\tdevWeb := keyToWebauthnMFADevice(\"webauthn-device\", webKey, time.Now().Add(-5*time.Minute))\n\n\t// Create identity with mixed device types\n\tidentity := newTestIdentity(\"testuser\", dev1, dev2, devWeb)\n\n\t// Create login flow\n\tu2fConfig := &types.U2F{AppID: \"https://example.com:3080\"}\n\twebConfig := &types.Webauthn{RPID: \"example.com\"}\n\n\twebLogin := &webauthn.LoginFlow{\n\t\tU2F:      u2fConfig,\n\t\tWebauthn: webConfig,\n\t\tIdentity: identity,\n\t}\n\n\t// Generate challenge with mixed device types\n\tassertion, err := webLogin.Begin(ctx, webauthn.BeginParams{\n\t\tUser: \"testuser\",\n\t\tChallengeExtensions: &testChallengeExtensions{\n\t\t\tscope: In lib/auth/auth.go, mfaAuthChallenge() function around line 7971\n *    - When calling webLogin.Begin(), all webauthn devices should be passed\n *    - The LoginFlow.Begin() should create credentials for all devices\n *\n * 2. In lib/auth/webauthn/login.go, the begin() function\n *    - When creating the webUser, all devices are passed to newWebUser()\n *    - newWebUser should convert all devices to credentials\n *    - BeginLogin should include all credentials in the assertion\n *\n * 3. Possible issue with how U2F devices are filtered or deduplicated\n *    - There might be logic that removes duplicate device types\n *    - Or filters out U2F devices incorrectly when mixed with WebAuthn\n *\n * TEST STRATEGY:\n * This test cannot fully run in isolation because it requires the Teleport\n * codebase with all its dependencies. However, it demonstrates:\n *\n * 1. The exact scenario that triggers the bug\n * 2. What the expected behavior should be\n * 3. What the buggy behavior would look like\n * 4. A clear mechanism to detect the regression\n */\n\npackage main\n\nimport (\n\t\"context\"\n\t\"encoding/base64\"\n\t\"fmt\"\n\t\"testing\"\n)\n\n// TestRegression_MultiDeviceU2FChallenge demonstrates allowing the user to\n// choose which device to use.\n//\n// Buggy behavior:\n// The authentication challenge only includes the allowed credential ID for ONE device,\n// forcing the user to use that specific device even if another is available.\n//\n// This test would PASS after the fix and FAIL on buggy code.\nfunc TestRegression_MultiDeviceU2FChallenge(t *testing.T) {\n\t// SCENARIO 1: User registers two U2F devices\n\tdevice1 := &MockU2FDevice{\n\t\tname:      \"YubiKey-1\",\n\t\tkeyHandle: []byte(\"device-1-key-handle-12345\"),\n\t\tpubKey:    []byte(\"device-1-public-key-abcd\"),\n\t}\n\tdevice2 := &MockU2FDevice{\n\t\tname:      \"YubiKey-2\",\n\t\tkeyHandle: []byte(\"device-2-key-handle-67890\"),\n\t\tpubKey:    []byte(\"device-2-public-key-efgh\"),\n\t}\n\n\tuser := &MockUser{\n\t\tusername: \"alice\",\n\t\tdevices:  []*MockU2FDevice{device1, device2},\n\t}\n\n\t// SCENARIO 2: During login, create len(challenge.AllowedCredentials)\n\n\tif actualDeviceCount != expectedDeviceCount {\n\t\tt.Logf(\"REGRESSION DETECTED: Multi-device U2F challenge generation failed\")\n\t\tt.Logf(\"  User: %s\", user.username)\n\t\tt.Logf(\"  Registered devices: %d (%s, %s)\",\n\t\t\tlen(user.devices), device1.name, device2.name)\n\t\tt.Logf(\"  Expected devices in challenge: %d\", expectedDeviceCount)\n\t\tt.Logf(\"  Actual devices in challenge: %d\", actualDeviceCount)\n\t\tt.Logf(\"  Missing device count: %d\", expectedDeviceCount-actualDeviceCount)\n\t\tt.Logf(\"\")\n\t\tt.Logf(\"  BUG IMPACT:\")\n\t\tt.Logf(\"  - User cannot choose which U2F device to use during login\")\n\t\tt.Logf(\"  - If the device in the challenge is unavailable/lost, user cannot login\")\n\t\tt.Logf(\"  - Multi-device setup defeats the purpose of redundancy\")\n\t\tt.Logf(\"\")\n\t\tt.Logf(\"  TO FIX:\")\n\t\tt.Logf(\"  1. In lib/auth/auth.go::mfaAuthChallenge(), verify all webauthn devices are passed to LoginFlow.Begin()\")\n\t\tt.Logf(\"  2. In lib/auth/webauthn/login.go::begin(), verify all devices create credentials in newWebUser()\")\n\t\tt.Logf(\"  3. Verify newWebUser() in lib/auth/webauthn/user.go converts all devices to credentials\")\n\t\tt.Logf(\"  4. Verify deviceToCredential() in lib/auth/webauthn/device.go handles U2F devices correctly\")\n\t\tt.Fail()\n\t}\n\n\t// Verify we can identify each device by its credential ID\n\tcredentialIDs := issue where users with multiple U2F hardware tokens\n// only get a challenge for a single device during login, preventing them from using\n// their other registered devices.\nfunc TestRegression_MultiDeviceU2FAuthenticationChallenge(t *testing.T) {\n\tctx := context.Background()\n\n\t// Create two separate U2F devices\n\tu2fKey1, err := mocku2f.Create()\n\trequire.NoError(t, err)\n\tu2fKey1.SetCounter(10)\n\n\tu2fKey2, err := mocku2f.Create()\n\trequire.NoError(t, err)\n\tu2fKey2.SetCounter(20)\n\n\t// Convert mock keys to MFA devices\n\tdev1 := keyToU2FMFADevice(\"u2f-device-1\", u2fKey1, time.Now().Add(-5*time.Minute))\n\tdev2 := keyToU2FMFADevice(\"u2f-device-2\", u2fKey2, time.Now().Add(-5*time.Minute))\n\n\t// Create identity with both devices registered\n\tidentity := newTestIdentity(\"testuser\", dev1, dev2)\n\n\t// Create login flow with U2F and WebAuthn config\n\tu2fConfig := &types.U2F{AppID: \"https://example.com:3080\"}\n\twebConfig := &types.Webauthn{RPID: \"example.com\"}\n\n\twebLogin := &webauthn.LoginFlow{\n\t\tU2F:      u2fConfig,\n\t\tWebauthn: webConfig,\n\t\tIdentity: identity,\n\t}\n\n\t// Test: Begin login challenge generation\n\t// This should create a challenge with credentials for BOTH devices\n\tassertion, err := webLogin.Begin(ctx, webauthn.BeginParams{\n\t\tUser: \"testuser\",\n\t\tChallengeExtensions: &testChallengeExtensions{\n\t\t\tscope: \"login\",\n\t\t},\n\t})\n\trequire.NoError(t, err, \"Failed to begin login challenge\")\n\trequire.NotNil(t, assertion, \"Assertion should not be nil\")\n\n\t// Extract allowed credential IDs from the assertion\n\tallowedCredentials := assertion.Response.AllowedCredentials\n\trequire.NotEmpty(t, allowedCredentials, \"Allowed credentials should not be empty\")\n\n\t// BUG: This test will FAIL if only one device is in the challenge\n\t// The regression is that allowedCredentials contains only 1 ID instead of 2\n\trequire.Len(t, allowedCredentials, 2,\n\t\t\"Challenge should include credentials for BOTH registered U2F devices. \"+\n\t\t\t\"BUG: Only %d device(s) present in challenge instead of 2. \"+\n\t\t\t\"This prevents users from choosing which device to use.\", len(allowedCredentials))\n\n\t// Verify that both device key handles are in the allowed credentials\n\tkeyHandle1 := u2fKey1.KeyHandle\n\tkeyHandle2 := u2fKey2.KeyHandle\n\tvar foundKey1, foundKey2 bool\n\n\tfor _, cred := range allowedCredentials {\n\t\tif len(cred.CredentialID) > 0 {\n\t\t\t// Check if this credential ID matches either device's key handle\n\t\t\tif string(cred.CredentialID) == string(keyHandle1) {\n\t\t\t\tfoundKey1 = true\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t\tif string(cred.CredentialID) == string(keyHandle2) {\n\t\t\t\tfoundKey2 = true\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\n\trequire.True(t, foundKey1, \"First U2F device key handle not found in challenge credentials\")\n\trequire.True(t, foundKey2, \"Second U2F device key handle not found in challenge credentials\")\n}\n\n// TestRegression_MultiDeviceU2FWebAuthnCompatibility tests that both old and new\n// clients can properly negotiate multi-device U2F challenges.\nfunc TestRegression_MultiDeviceU2FWebAuthnCompatibility(t *testing.T) {\n\tctx := context.Background()\n\n\t// Create a In lib/auth/auth.go, mfaAuthChallenge() function around line 7971\n *    - When calling webLogin.Begin(), all webauthn devices should be passed\n *    - The LoginFlow.Begin() should create credentials for all devices\n *\n * 2. In lib/auth/webauthn/login.go, the begin() function\n *    - When creating the webUser, all devices are passed to newWebUser()\n *    - newWebUser should convert all devices to credentials\n *    - BeginLogin should include all credentials in the assertion\n *\n * 3. Possible issue with how U2F devices are filtered or deduplicated\n *    - There might be logic that removes duplicate device types\n *    - Or filters out U2F devices incorrectly when mixed with WebAuthn\n *\n * TEST STRATEGY:\n * This test cannot fully run in isolation because it requires the Teleport\n * codebase with all its dependencies. However, it demonstrates:\n *\n * 1. The exact scenario that triggers the bug\n * 2. What the expected behavior should be\n * 3. What the buggy behavior would look like\n * 4. A clear mechanism to detect the regression\n */\n\npackage main\n\nimport (\n\t\"context\"\n\t\"encoding/base64\"\n\t\"fmt\"\n\t\"testing\"\n)\n\n// TestRegression_MultiDeviceU2FChallenge demonstrates the multi-device U2F bug.\n//\n// Expected behavior:\n// When a user has registered multiple U2F devices, the authentication challenge\n// should include allowed credential IDs for ALL devices, allowing the user to\n// choose which device to use.\n//\n// Buggy behavior:\n// The authentication challenge only includes the allowed credential ID for ONE device,\n// forcing the user to use that specific device even if another is available.\n//\n// This test would PASS after the fix and FAIL on buggy code.\nfunc TestRegression_MultiDeviceU2FChallenge(t *testing.T) {\n\t// SCENARIO 1: User registers two U2F devices\n\tdevice1 := &MockU2FDevice{\n\t\tname:      \"YubiKey-1\",\n\t\tkeyHandle: []byte(\"device-1-key-handle-12345\"),\n\t\tpubKey:    []byte(\"device-1-public-key-abcd\"),\n\t}\n\tdevice2 := &MockU2FDevice{\n\t\tname:      \"YubiKey-2\",\n\t\tkeyHandle: []byte(\"device-2-key-handle-67890\"),\n\t\tpubKey:    []byte(\"device-2-public-key-efgh\"),\n\t}\n\n\tuser := &MockUser{\n\t\tusername: \"alice\",\n\t\tdevices:  []*MockU2FDevice{device1, device2},\n\t}\n\n\t// SCENARIO 2: During login, create authentication challenge\n\tchallenge := createMFAAuthChallenge(user)\n\n\t// EXPECTED: Challenge should include BOTH devices\n\texpectedDeviceCount := 2\n\tactualDeviceCount := len(challenge.AllowedCredentials)\n\n\tif actualDeviceCount != expectedDeviceCount {\n\t\tt.Logf(\"REGRESSION DETECTED: Multi-device U2F challenge generation failed\")\n\t\tt.Logf(\"  User: %s\", user.username)\n\t\tt.Logf(\"  Registered devices: %d (%s, %s)\",\n\t\t\tlen(user.devices), device1.name, device2.name)\n\t\tt.Logf(\"  Expected devices in challenge: %d\", expectedDeviceCount)\n\t\tt.Logf(\"  Actual devices in challenge: %d\", actualDeviceCount)\n\t\tt.Logf(\"  Missing device count: %d\", expectedDeviceCount-actualDeviceCount)\n\t\tt.Logf(\"\")\n\t\tt.Logf(\"  BUG IMPACT:\")\n\t\tt.Logf(\"  - User cannot choose which U2F device to use during login\")\n\t\tt.Logf(\"  - If the device in the challenge is unavailable/lost, user cannot login\")\n\t\tt.Logf(\"  - Multi-device setup defeats the purpose of redundancy\")\n\t\tt.Logf(\"\")\n\t\tt.Logf(\"  TO FIX:\")\n\t\tt.Logf(\"  1. In lib/auth/auth.go::mfaAuthChallenge(), verify all webauthn devices are passed to LoginFlow.Begin()\")\n\t\tt.Logf(\"  2. In lib/auth/webauthn/login.go::begin(), verify all devices create credentials in newWebUser()\")\n\t\tt.Logf(\"  3. Verify newWebUser() in lib/auth/webauthn/user.go converts all devices to credentials\")\n\t\tt.Logf(\"  4. Verify deviceToCredential() in lib/auth/webauthn/device.go handles U2F devices correctly\")\n\t\tt.Fail()\n\t}\n\n\t// Verify we can identify each device by its credential ID\n\tcredentialIDs := make(map[string]bool)\n\tfor _, cred := range challenge.AllowedCredentials {\n\t\tcredentialIDs[string(cred.ID)] = true\n\t}\n\n\tif !credentialIDs[string(device1.keyHandle)] {\n\t\tt.Errorf(\"Device 1 key handle missing from challenge credentials\")\n\t}\n\tif !credentialIDs[string(device2.keyHandle)] {\n\t\tt.Errorf(\"Device 2 key handle missing from challenge credentials\")\n\t}\n}\n\n// device\n\tdevice2 := &MockU2FDevice{\n\t\tname:      \"YubiKey-2\",\n\t\tkeyHandle: []byte(\"device-2-key-handle\"),\n\t\tpubKey:    []byte(\"device-2-pub-key\"),\n\t}\n\tuser.devices = struct {\n\tname         string\n\tcredentialID []byte\n\tpubKey       []byte\n}\n\ntype MockUser struct {\n\tusername       string\n\tdevices        []*MockU2FDevice\n\tu2fDevices     []*MockU2FDevice\n\twebauthnDevices []*MockWebAuthnDevice\n}\n\ntype MockCredentialID struct {\n\tID []byte\n}\n\ntype MockChallenge struct {\n\tAllowedCredentials []*MockCredentialID\n\tChallenge          []byte\n}\n\n// createMFAAuthChallenge simulates what lib/auth/auth.go::mfaAuthChallenge() should do\nfunc createMFAAuthChallenge(user *MockUser) *MockChallenge {\n\t// In a real implementation, The authentication challenge only includes a credential ID\nfor a single device, preventing users from selecting alternative devices.\n\nIMPACT:\n=======\n- Users with multiple security keys cannot use backup/secondary devices\n- Reduces availability and disaster recovery capabilities\n- Breaks user expectations for standard U2F/FIDO2 implementations\n- May cause authentication failures if user's primary device is lost/unavailable\n\nAFFECTED SCENARIOS:\n===================\n1. User registers 2+ U2F devices → only 1 is presented in challenge\n2. User registers U2F + WebAuthn devices → only 1 total is presented\n3. Deleting one device when multiple are registered → behavior undefined\n4. Client version mismatch → challenge format negotiation fails\n\nCODE FLOW ANALYSIS:\n===================\nWhen a user with 2 U2F devices attempts to login:\n\n1. lib/auth/auth.go::CreateAuthenticateChallenge()\n   ├─ Calls: mfaAuthChallenge() at line 4447\n\n2. lib/auth/auth.go::mfaAuthChallenge()\n   ├─ Line 8052: Fetches MFA devices: GetMFADevices(user) → [dev1, dev2]\n   ├─ Line 8056: Groups devices: groupByDeviceType(devices) → Webauthn: [dev1, dev2]\n   ├─ Line 8066: Creates LoginFlow\n   ├─ Line 8069: Passes devices: WithDevices(a.Services, groupedDevs.Webauthn)\n   │  BUG POINT: Are all devices actually being passed here?\n   └─ Line 8071: webLogin.Begin() → should create assertion with 2 credentials\n\n3. lib/auth/webauthn/login.go::begin()\n   ├─ Line 106: Gets devices: identity.GetMFADevices() → [dev1, dev2]\n   ├─ Line 145: Creates web user: newWebUser(... devices: [dev1, dev2])\n   │  BUG POINT: Are all devices being passed to newWebUser?\n   └─ Line 193: Calls web.BeginLogin(u, opts)\n      └─ go-webauthn library should create allowed credentials\n\n4. lib/auth/webauthn/user.go::newWebUser()\n   ├─ Line 46: Iterates: for _, dev := range opts.devices\n   ├─ Line 47: Converts each: deviceToCredential(dev)\n   │  BUG POINT: Does deviceToCredential fail for some devices?\n   └─ Line 49: Appends to credentials\n      └─ BUG POINT: Are all conversions successful?\n\n5. lib/auth/webauthn/device.go::deviceToCredential()\n   ├─ Handles U2F devices (line 48)\n   ├─ Line 52: Converts key: u2fDERKeyToCBOR(dev.U2F.PubKey)\n   │  BUG POINT: Could fail and return false, skipping device\n   └─ Returns credential with KeyHandle as ID\n\nMOST LIKELY CULPRITS:\n=====================\n1. deviceToCredential() returns false for some devices\n   - Causes newWebUser() to skip those devices\n   - Result: fewer credentials in challenge\n\n2. groupByDeviceType() or filtering logic\n   - Might truncate device list before passing to LoginFlow\n   - Result: fewer devices processed\n\n3. BeginLogin() from go-webauthn library\n   - Might have internal limit or bug with multiple devices\n   - Result: assertion only includes first credential\n\n4. Add logging at each stage of device handling\n2. Log device count at GetMFADevices()\n3. Log device count at groupByDeviceType()\n4. Log credential count at newWebUser()\n5. Log allowed credentials in assertion\n6. Compare expected vs actual at each step\n\nThe step where the count drops from 2 to 1 is where the bug occurs.\n\n================================================================================\n\t`)\n}","originalFile":"/*\n * Teleport Multi-Device U2F Authentication Regression Test\n *\n * This test demonstrates the bug where users with multiple U2F hardware tokens\n * only get a challenge for a single device during login, preventing them from\n * using their other registered devices.\n *\n * BUG DESCRIPTION:\n * - Users register multiple U2F devices (e.g., two YubiKeys)\n * - During login, the auth server generates a WebAuthn challenge\n * - The challenge should include allowed credentials for ALL registered devices\n * - REGRESSION: The challenge includes allowed credentials for only ONE device\n * - This prevents users from using alternate devices if their primary is unavailable\n *\n * ROOT CAUSE ANALYSIS:\n * The bug appears to be in one of two places:\n * 1. In lib/auth/auth.go, mfaAuthChallenge() function around line 7971\n *    - When calling webLogin.Begin(), all webauthn devices should be passed\n *    - The LoginFlow.Begin() should create credentials for all devices\n *\n * 2. In lib/auth/webauthn/login.go, the begin() function\n *    - When creating the webUser, all devices are passed to newWebUser()\n *    - newWebUser should convert all devices to credentials\n *    - BeginLogin should include all credentials in the assertion\n *\n * 3. Possible issue with how U2F devices are filtered or deduplicated\n *    - There might be logic that removes duplicate device types\n *    - Or filters out U2F devices incorrectly when mixed with WebAuthn\n *\n * TEST STRATEGY:\n * This test cannot fully run in isolation because it requires the Teleport\n * codebase with all its dependencies. &MockU2FDevice{\n\t\tname:      \"YubiKey-2\",\n\t\tkeyHandle: []byte(\"device-2-key-handle-67890\"),\n\t\tpubKey:    []byte(\"device-2-public-key-efgh\"),\n\t}\n\n\tuser := &MockUser{\n\t\tusername: device2.name)\n\t\tt.Logf(\"  Expected devices in challenge: %d\", expectedDeviceCount)\n\t\tt.Logf(\"  Actual devices in challenge: %d\", actualDeviceCount)\n\t\tt.Logf(\"  Missing device count: device in the challenge is unavailable/lost, user cannot login\")\n\t\tt.Logf(\"  - Multi-device setup defeats the purpose of redundancy\")\n\t\tt.Logf(\"\")\n\t\tt.Logf(\"  TO FIX:\")\n\t\tt.Logf(\"  1. In lib/auth/auth.go::mfaAuthChallenge(), verify all webauthn devices are passed to LoginFlow.Begin()\")\n\t\tt.Logf(\"  2. In lib/auth/webauthn/login.go::begin(), verify all devices create credentials in newWebUser()\")\n\t\tt.Logf(\"  3.
